- `8` **Exclusive: ServiceNow to cut up to 1K jobs as part of planned rightsizing - Yahoo Finance**
  Reported new fact: ServiceNow will cut up to 1,000 jobs in a "planned rightsizing" — the first sizable headcount reduction disclosed at NOW, against a company that has historically guided to hiring alongside 20%+ subscription growth. For coverage this is a NOW margin/narrative datapoint rather than a demand datapoint: cost actions can support FY operating-margin guidance even if net-new ACV softens, so the read is ambiguous until management frames it as AI-driven productivity (bullish on opex leverage) versus slowing bookings (bearish). Confirmation would be the next quarterly guide holding subscription revenue growth while raising margin; refutation would be a cut alongside a trimmed cRPO/subscription outlook. Treat as incremental unless paired with a guidance change. *(headline only)*

- `8` **Nvidia (NVDA) Could Backstop $250 Billion for OpenAI. Where Does That Leave Microsoft (MSFT)? - Yahoo Finance**
  Report frames Nvidia as potentially backstopping up to $250B of OpenAI obligations, and asks what that leaves for Microsoft — the strategic point being that if NVDA, not MSFT, underwrites OpenAI's compute, Microsoft loses its privileged position as OpenAI's capacity landlord/financier. For coverage this touches openai-xpu and hyperscaler-guides: OpenAI capacity funded via Nvidia credit rather than Azure capex implies OpenAI's incremental compute skews to Nvidia GPUs and neoclouds, which is mildly negative for the custom-XPU thesis timing at AVGO (OpenAI accelerator program) and for reading MSFT capex as the OpenAI demand proxy into the late-July guide window. Numbers here are press speculation, not disclosure — treat as unconfirmed. Confirmation would be contractual detail in NVDA/OpenAI filings or a Microsoft comment on reduced OpenAI exclusivity. *(headline only)*

- `8` **Meta net income falls 14% to $15.8bn as costs rise in Q2 2026 - verdict.co.uk**
  Meta's Q2 2026 net income fell 14% to $15.8bn as costs rose — a margin-compression print driven by expense growth, which is the P&L signature of the AI infrastructure build rather than a demand problem (the excerpt gives no revenue or capex figure, so the capex guide itself is unconfirmed here). For meta-capex/meta-ai and the AI supply chain (NVDA, AVGO, VRT, CRWV, MRVL), the read-through is that Meta is still absorbing depreciation and compute opex rather than throttling spend; earnings dilution at the customer is historically the precondition for continued orders, not their cancellation. Watch the accompanying capex guidance and depreciation schedule in the release: an unchanged-or-raised 2026/2027 capex number alongside the 14% EPS-side decline confirms "spend through the margin hit" and supports the semis/power complex; a capex trim with cost discipline language would be the first genuine hyperscaler-guide crack and negative for VRT/NBIS/CRWV. Also relevant to the sell-the-beat question on TSM — if Meta pairs falling profit with flat capex, the buyside debate shifts from demand to who pays for it. *(headline only)*

- `8` **Microsoft Committed $190B CapEx and Pulled SanDisk Out of Their Slump - Yahoo Finance**
  The report ties SanDisk's move out of its slump to Microsoft committing $190B of capex — a very large absolute number that, if it is a multi-year cumulative commitment rather than an annual guide, is not directly comparable to prior annual hyperscaler figures; the excerpt does not specify the period, so treat the magnitude as unverified. For hyperscaler-guides and SNDK, the mechanism is that hyperscaler storage tiers (nearline/QLC eSSD for AI data lakes and checkpointing) are now being underwritten by named capex rather than spot demand, which is what turns NAND from a cyclical price-taker into a contracted-volume business. Confirms the nand-cycle and hbf-flash theses if Microsoft or SanDisk disclose long-term supply agreements or prepayments; refuted if the $190B proves to be a restatement of already-known commitments with no storage-specific allocation. Read alongside items 4/5 — the same tape is pricing supply scarcity, so the risk is that SNDK's move is sentiment-led ahead of realized contract pricing. *(headline only)*

- `8` **Teradyne's Q2 net revenue reached $1.33 billions, up 104% compared to Q2 2025. Among this, semiconductor test business revenue was $1.122 billions, product test business revenue was $107 millions, and robotics business revenue was $100 millions. - Bitget**
  The granular split: 2Q net revenue $1.33B with semiconductor test $1.122B (84% of total), product test $107M and robotics $100M — so essentially all of the growth is semi test, and robotics remains a ~$100M/quarter rounding error rather than the thesis. The cited '+104% vs Q2 2025' comparison looks garbled (a doubling of total revenue year-over-year is implausible versus TER's recent run-rate); treat the mix data as reliable and the growth rate as unverified. Decision-useful point: an 84% semi-test mix means TER is now effectively a levered AI compute/HBM test play, tightening its correlation to NVDA/AVGO/MRVL accelerator volumes and to 6857 as the HBM comparator (hbm-test). Confirm via the segment detail in the actual release; if semi test alone is up triple digits, that is an inflection datapoint on test intensity, not incremental news. *(headline only)*

- `8` **Samsung warns memory crunch will last through 2028 as profit rises 19 - fold**
  Samsung guided the memory shortage out an extra two years: EVP Jaejune Kim said supply constraints will be 'even more severe in 2027 than 2026' and that it is 'unlikely to see any significant increase in incremental supply through 2028,' because fab-to-wafer lead times exceed three years — while posting Q2 revenue of ₩171.5T ($119B) +130% y/y and operating profit of ₩89.5T ($62.4B), more than 19x the year-ago figure. Next-quarter bit growth is only mid-single-digit DRAM / high-single-digit NAND, and Samsung is explicitly rationing: multiyear supply agreements go to 'those who can guarantee committed future captive demand' (SK Hynix said the same thing the same day), with HBM4/HBM4E, DDR5, SOCAMM2 and eSSDs the priority mix. This extends the duration leg of the memory pricing-power thesis — the debate has moved from level to duration — supporting 000660, SNDK and 285A, and it is the same force cutting Arm's smartphone unit forecast and inflating Advantest's component costs. Sources disagree on NAND specifically: Samsung frames the crunch as through-2028 across memory, while TrendForce expects NAND supply to *ease* in 2H27 on layer-count migration and new fabs, and sees DRAM's new 2027 lines not ramping meaningfully until 2H27–2028 — so the DRAM/HBM call is more defensible than the NAND call for SNDK. Refutation to watch: any spot/contract DRAM price rollover, or hyperscalers publicly resisting multiyear pricing; confirmation would be further multiyear deal announcements and a Q3 capex plan that still doesn't add 2027 wafer capacity.

- `8` **  최대 실적  SK하이닉스 , 중장기 메모리 수요 확신 … LTA · 투자 확대 나선다**
  SK Hynix posted record but consensus-missing Q2: revenue 79.32tn won vs 83.94tn expected and OP 60.54tn vs 63.99tn (net income 93.92tn beat the 51.58tn consensus on non-operating items), with revenue +257% YoY and +51% QoQ. The pricing detail is the story: DRAM ASP +30% QoQ and NAND ASP up mid-50% QoQ, on only high-single-digit DRAM and mid-teens NAND bit growth — i.e. essentially all of the upside is price, which is exactly the pricing-power regime the LTA structure locks in (10-plus customers signed, typically five-year terms; 2027 volume and price talks already "progressing smoothly"). Capex steps up to high-40tn won from 30.17tn (+15tn+), with M15X pulled forward, Yongin Y1 cleanroom opening early next year, Y1 ph1 tool orders already placed this month, a February one-pass line and March–April setup for 20k wpm, plus P&T7 advanced packaging and M17 NAND — a hard, dated order calendar for AMAT, LRCX, KLA, ONTO, CAMT, TER and 6857/Advantest, and for BESI given explicit hybrid-bonding and iHBM (>30% lower thermal resistance) development. HBM4 entered mass production in Q2 with yield/quality already near mature HBM3E and HBM4E samples shipped for volume next year, which refutes the bear case that HBM4 ramp slips; the offset for 000660 holders is that an OP miss against a red-hot ASP backdrop is precisely the "sell-the-beat" setup, so watch whether Q3 guidance and 2027 HBM pricing agreements are disclosed with volume commitments attached.
  Sources: [zdnet.co.kr](https://zdnet.co.kr/view/?no=20260729122141)
- `8` **With 8,192 Ascend Chips and Optical Interconnect, Huawei's Atlas 950 SuperPod Aims to Bypass U.S. Sanctions - Windows News**
  Huawei's Atlas 950 SuperPod, unveiled at WAIC 2026, clusters 8,192 Ascend 950DT chips over an all-optical fabric with shipment stated for Q4 2026; note the source contradicts itself on performance, claiming '8 exaFLOPS' in one place and 'up to 16 EFLOPS at FP4' in another, so treat the throughput number as unverified. The mechanism is explicit scale-out substitution: many domestic dies plus optical interconnect to compensate for per-chip and HBM disadvantages under export controls (huawei-ascend, export-controls). For coverage, the interesting second-order read is optics and interconnect intensity — an 8,192-chip all-optical pod implies very high EML/optical-engine and InP wafer content per rack, which supports the china-optics and inp-capacity theses (IQE, LITE, AAOI, CBRS demand pull, but sourced domestically where possible) and validates optical scale-up as an alternative to copper NVLink/UALink (mrvl-fabric). This is an aggregator item with thin sourcing; confirm with Huawei's own spec sheet, Ascend 950DT HBM sourcing (CXMT progress), and any Q4 2026 delivery evidence — refute if shipment slips or volumes stay sub-scale, in which case it stays a sanctions-narrative headline rather than a demand inflection.
  Sources: [windowsnews.ai](https://windowsnews.ai/article/with-8192-ascend-chips-and-optical-interconnect-huaweis-atlas-950-superpod-aims-to-bypass-us-sanctio.440928)
- `8` **Wall St Engine on X: "TSMC DEVELOPS EMIB-LIKE PACKAGING $TSM is working with substrate maker Kinsus on a silicon-bridge technology designed to support larger multi-die AI chips and provide an alternative to its capacity-constrained CoWoS process. The project remains in its early stages, with no comm**
  The Information reports TSMC is working with substrate maker Kinsus on a silicon-bridge (EMIB-like) packaging path as an alternative to capacity-constrained CoWoS, but the project is early-stage with no commercial launch timeline. The harder numbers are on the Intel side: Nvidia is said to be evaluating EMIB for a future processor combining four GPUs, and Google has reportedly ordered Intel to package more than 3 million custom AI processors in 2028 — if accurate, that is an inflection datapoint for intel-18a/advanced packaging rather than incremental news, because packaging is the wedge that could pull front-end wafer orders to Intel later. Sources disagree on framing: MarketArena argues TSMC already has silicon bridges via CoWoS-L, so the Kinsus work is about capacity, cost and flexibility, not a technology gap — which would make it TSM-neutral and read more as CoWoS supply tightness (touches cowos, copos-panel, tsmc-capex). Confirmation would be Kinsus capex/qualification disclosures or Intel Foundry naming Google/Nvidia packaging volumes on a call; refutation would be Nvidia sticking to CoWoS-L for its four-GPU part.
  Sources: [x.com](https://x.com/wallstengine/status/2082814426848325887)
- `8` **2027 wird noch schlimmer: 70 Prozent der Samsung-Speicherproduktion in 5-Jahres-LTAs - ComputerBase**
  Samsung told investors up to 70% of its memory output will sit under long-term agreements averaging five years with a "rolling" auto-extension structure — five large customers signed, five more close, further inquiries pending — and repeated that the shortage gets worse in 2027, with 2028 barely better because new capacity only comes online slowly from 2028; it declined to forecast beyond 2029. Samsung guides HBM4 revenue to more than triple q/q in Q3, expects 2H HBM share similar to its DRAM share, confirms AMD's MI455X uses Samsung HBM4, and notes over 60% of NAND revenue is already server SSDs. This is the inflection framing for the memory complex: duration and visibility (bullish 000660, 285A, SNDK volumes and semicap capex) purchased by giving up spot upside, mirroring Micron's and SK Hynix's LTA strategy — which is exactly why record prints are being sold. The MI455X/Samsung HBM4 link also de-risks amd-parity supply; watch whether the remaining five LTAs are signed and whether any customer's pricing terms leak, since fixed-price duration is what would cap 2027 EPS revisions despite a worsening shortage.
  Sources: [computerbase.de](https://computerbase.de/news/wirtschaft/2027-wird-noch-schlimmer-70-prozent-der-samsung-speicherproduktion-in-5-jahres-ltas.98637)

- `7` **Intel Cracks The Encapsulation Wall Blocking Hyper-Large Chips, Pushing Advanced Packaging To 24x Reticle Size and Beyond - Wccftech**
  Intel claims to have solved the molding/encapsulation warpage limit that constrains very large packages, enabling advanced packaging at up to 24x reticle size and beyond — a step-change versus current CoWoS-class packages roughly in the 3.5–5.5x reticle range. If real and manufacturable, this is the substantive technical counter to item 1/2's TSMC bridge report and directly supports the intel-18a/foundry thesis: packaging, not the 18A node, is where Intel can win AI sockets first, and 24x reticle implies far more HBM stacks and compute dielets per package. Second-order reads: bullish for HBM demand per accelerator (000660, samsung-hbm/hbm4 questions), for panel-level/CoPoS and glass-substrate adoption since organic substrates warp badly at that size, and for warpage/inspection metrology (ONTO, CAMT) and die-attach/hybrid-bonding tools (BESI, ASMI). Confirmation requires a named external foundry customer or a disclosed product using it; absent that, treat as a research-paper-stage claim rather than a bookable capacity inflection. *(headline only)*

  CXMT topped market cap on its STAR Market debut (Chosun), giving China's leading DRAM maker a cheap, deep domestic equity funding channel. Mechanism that matters for coverage: listing proceeds plus a high valuation lower CXMT's cost of capital for DDR5/1c-DRAM and HBM capacity adds, which is the main structural threat to the conventional-DRAM leg of the memory upcycle behind item 1 (000660, and Samsung/Micron in the samsung-hbm question). - `7` **Circle clears final hurdle to establish national trust bank with OCC approval - CryptoRank**
  Circle received OCC approval clearing the final hurdle to establish a national trust bank. That converts USDC reserve custody and institutional servicing from a bank-dependent arrangement into a federally chartered entity, which structurally lowers counterparty/regulatory risk for large treasurers and, by implication, widens the addressable institutional float that drives Circle's reserve-interest revenue and its negotiating position with distribution partners. For CRCL this is the key regulatory de-risking datapoint of the cycle and it also strengthens the settlement leg of the agentic-commerce/x402 thesis, since regulated custody is what enterprise merchants and AI-agent platforms cite as the blocker to programmatic stablecoin payments (inference). - `7` **The Kumamoto earthquake in Japan has resulted in 28 fatalities; Taiwan Semiconductor’s (TSM.US) first Kumamoto fab will require time to resume normal production. - news.futunn.com**
  Follow-on reporting puts the Kumamoto quake death toll at 28 and says TSMC's first Kumamoto fab (JASM Fab 1) will need time to resume normal production — the key new fact versus the initial evacuation headline is that this is a restart-with-delay, not a same-day recovery, which implies tool re-qualification and possible wafer scrap. For TSM the earnings impact should be immaterial given JASM's small share of consolidated wafers, but there are two live second-order paths: mature-node/auto chip tightness (supportive for specialty-node pricing at TSEM, and a cost/availability risk for auto and image-sensor customers), and capex timing for JASM Fab 2 construction. Also watch Kyushu-based equipment and materials operations (6857, 5332, 6981) for their own disruption disclosures. - `7` **Samsung expands Texas operations as global memory crunch worsens through 2028**
  Samsung explicitly guided that the memory shortage will "intensify in 2027 and persist into 2028," citing a >3.5-year lead time from fab construction to wafer out — the most concrete multi-year tightness statement yet from the largest supplier, and an inflection-grade datapoint rather than incremental commentary. It is backing this with contracts, not words: five-year rolling LTAs signed with five global datacenter operators plus five more AI customers in final-stage talks, backed by upfront payments and intended to cover 60–70% of medium/long-term output — structurally de-cycling the memory book and directly supportive of 000660, SNDK and 285A pricing power. Q2 was record (revenue 171.5tn won/$119.2bn, +130% YoY; OP 89.5tn won, +1,813.8%; DS division 89.2tn won OP), but note the tell on the other side of the cost curve: the device division lost 800bn won on 48tn won of revenue purely on component cost inflation — the clearest evidence that memory tightness is now destroying downstream hardware margins. Also two second-order reads: Taylor Fab 1 comes online this year on 2nm with Fab 2 breaking ground by year-end (mass production 2030), a direct US-capacity comparison for tsmc-nodes/TSM Arizona; and Samsung ruled out a Nasdaq ADR, removing an overhang/comp for the 000660 ADR premium. Watch H2 HBM4 shipment ramp disclosures to confirm the mix-driven profit guide.
  Sources: [aninews.in](https://aninews.in/news/business/samsung-expands-texas-operations-as-global-memory-crunch-worsens-through-202820260730182509/)
- `7` **Amazon Zoox wins first US approval for paid robotaxis without human controls**
  NHTSA granted Zoox the first-ever US exemption from human-control requirements for paid robotaxi service, permitting up to 2,500 purpose-built vehicles annually for two years (75 mph, inward-facing seats, Las Vegas and San Francisco, remote operators required to be US-based, mandatory crash/improper-stop reporting and published operating maps). For UBER this is a third at-scale network — Amazon-funded — that has no reason to route through Uber's demand aggregation, arriving in the same week as reports Waymo may exit its Uber tie-up; the 2,500/yr cap is small versus Waymo's fleet, so near-term ride-supply impact is limited, but the regulatory precedent is the inflection: purpose-built, no-steering-wheel vehicles now have a federal pathway, which lowers the per-mile cost floor Uber's human-driver network must compete against. NHTSA also flagged it can pull the exemption, cited a "clear pattern" of AVs interfering with first responders, and Zoox already recalled 105 vehicles for a software fix — so the read refutes if enforcement action or a further recall stalls deployment. Next date to watch: NHTSA's stated intent to publish the first federal ADS safety standards by the end of this administration, plus state/local approvals needed before Zoox can charge fares.
  Sources: [Amy Stillman and Kara Carlson](https://www.ttnews.com/articles/amazon-zoox-5000-robotaxis) · [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMitgFBVV95cUxNWnc0dGM0Q3ViQk5QbzEtVXRJeFhHSG5rMGlGQXk1TC1sT3lIRHVjRVpPbUdTdDRkOHVTNk94dnV3RF80TlA1M0xnZHZZcXRwenlhaVBtTlJqcm1fVlpOS1Etb3c2TmZtNXhHZmozZDZPNWp5NHgxazUtQkxieXg5WmIxLUFKRWlsWDVyN2txWlBYOHZXMlVISHhZYlhvRVZIU25KY3E5dlZBLUJYRnRRY0ROX2Ztdw?oc=5) · [wral.com](https://www.wral.com/news/ap/1bdb3-some-robotaxis-approved-to-operate-without-steering-wheels-and-losing-the-brake-pedal-could-be-next/) · [dailylocal.com](https://www.dailylocal.com/2026/07/30/robotaxis-without-steering-wheels/) · [tribune.com.pk](https://tribune.com.pk/story/2621151/amazons-zoox-wins-first-us-approval-for-paid-robotaxis-without-human-controls) · [wokv.com](https://www.wokv.com/news/business/some-robotaxis/5REPULH7E45KNMZZW6JPBSMHY4/) · [thenews.com.pk](https://www.thenews.com.pk/latest/1410722-zoox-wins-us-approval-for-steering-wheel-free-robotaxis)
- `7` **Waymo may end Uber robotaxi alliance , FT reports**
  The FT reports Waymo has held internal discussions about terminating its 2023 Uber partnership, citing disputes over vehicle cleanliness and Uber routing (Waymo's complaints) versus weather-driven supply withdrawal and "unsustainable financial terms" (Uber's), and says Waymo has told Uber it intends to enter the affected markets independently from January 2028, the first date the agreement permits; the Phoenix partnership already ended in late June, leaving Austin and Atlanta. Uber closed down 4.3%, which is the market pricing the loss of Uber's core AV bridge thesis — that it aggregates demand for third-party autonomy rather than competing with it. Combined with Zoox's NHTSA exemption, the transmission for UBER is that both leading US robotaxi operators now have credible direct-to-consumer paths, compressing the option value in Uber's marketplace multiple even while 2026-27 earnings are unaffected. Confirmation: formal notice of termination or Waymo app-only expansion announcements in Austin/Atlanta; refutation: renegotiated economics or a new city added to the Uber-Waymo arrangement. Note the report is single-sourced and unverified, and neither company commented.
  Sources: [nigeriasun.com](http://www.nigeriasun.com/news/279209550/waymo-may-end-uber-robotaxi-alliance-ft-reports) · [bruneinews.net](http://www.bruneinews.net/news/279209550/waymo-explores-ending-uber-deal-amid-growing-tensions) · [bignewsnetwork.com](http://www.bignewsnetwork.com/news/279209550/waymo-weighs-ending-uber-partnership-report-says)
- `7` **OpenAI Jalapeno Chip Explained: Specs, Cost, Release Date**
  New specifics on the OpenAI/Broadcom inference ASIC 'Jalapeño': unveiled June 24, 2026 with a physical wafer holding only ~50–60 dies (implying a reticle-limited, very large die), eight HBM stacks in one package, a systolic-array architecture optimized for memory bandwidth rather than peak TFLOPS, TSMC 3nm, Broadcom Tomahawk Ultra Ethernet for scale-out, and Celestica as systems integrator; OpenAI claims ~50% lower inference cost vs the GPUs it uses today, and Hock Tan calls OpenAI demand 'insatiable' through 2028. For openai-xpu this is the most quantitative disclosure yet and it is bullish AVGO XPU revenue, TSM N3 loading, and — because eight HBM stacks per package is Rubin-class memory content — HBM4/HBM demand at 000660 and 285A plus CoWoS/advanced-packaging capacity (cowos, hbm4). Read-across: an inference-only ASIC at 50% lower cost attacks Nvidia's highest-volume future workload rather than training, which is the share-shift path the custom-silicon thesis needs, and it raises the bar for MRVL to land a comparable flagship socket. The 50% claim is a vendor number with no independent benchmark — confirm via Broadcom's next XPU guide and any third-party perf/W data; refute if volume ramp dates slip or if the part is confined to internal ChatGPT serving.
  Sources: [mayhemcode.com](https://mayhemcode.com/2026/07/blog-post.html)
- `7` **Meta earnings analysis: questions answered and next catalysts By Investing.com**
  Meta narrowed FY26 capex upward to $130–145B from $125–145B (floor +$5B, midpoint from ~$135B to ~$137.5B), and days before the print announced a $14B AI data center JV with BlackRock in El Paso — off-balance-sheet financing that means reported capex understates total compute buildout. For meta-capex and the hyperscaler-guides question in the Jul 28–31 window, this is an upward-but-not-shock revision: the floor raise removes the 'capex digestion' scenario for 2027 order books at NVDA, VRT, AVGO (MTIA), CRWV and the semicap chain, while the JV structure signals Meta will keep spending via SPVs even if the market punishes headline capex. Read this alongside Alphabet's -7% capex-raise reaction and Meta's own -9% after-hours move: demand for AI supply-chain names is intact, but the equity market is now taxing the spenders rather than the suppliers, which is exactly the 'sell-the-beat' asymmetry to watch on TSM. Confirmation would be El Paso ground-breaking/equipment orders and a 2027 capex framing above $145B on the next call; refutation would be the JV substituting for, rather than adding to, owned capacity. *(headline only)*

- `7` **SK Hynix Stock Rises Overnight Even as Company Misses Profit Estimates - SK hynix (NASDAQ:SKHY) - Benzinga**
  The operational detail behind the miss is more bullish than the tape: SK Hynix guided 2026 capex to the high end of its KRW 40T range (AI memory capacity plus advanced packaging), Q3 DRAM shipments +~10% and NAND +~3% q/q, FY26 demand growth of ~25% DRAM and ~18% NAND, a record 76% operating margin, ~10 finalized long-term supply agreements with more multi-year talks underway, HBM4 in mass shipment, HBM4E customer sampling complete, and M15X/Yongin capacity ramping. Note net profit (KRW 93.92T) exceeded revenue (KRW 79.32T) on non-operating gains — a reminder that the headline "record" is flattered and that the operating miss is the real number. Capex to the top of range plus advanced-packaging spend is a direct read-through to AMAT, LRCX, KLA, ONTO, CAMT, TER, 6857 (Advantest, hbm-test) and BESI (hybrid-bonding), while ~18% NAND demand growth and server-SSD mix support SNDK. Refutation to watch: any trim of the 40T capex or slippage in HBM4E qualification, since HBM4E sampling completion is the gate for 2027 share defense against Samsung.
  Sources: [www.benzinga.com](https://www.benzinga.com/markets/earnings/26/07/60748826/sk-hynix-q2-earnings-ai-demand-capex-jim-cramer-skhy-stock-rises-after-hours)
- `7` **Samsung: HBM4 to Exceed 60% of HBM Sales in Second Half - Seoul Economic Daily**
  On its Q2 call Samsung said HBM4 will exceed 60% of total HBM sales in 2H26 and that it plans to run HBM share at roughly the same level as its commodity DRAM share — an explicit parity ambition against market leader SK Hynix, backed by Samsung's claim to be first to mass-production ship HBM4 (February). HBM4 is the part going into Nvidia's Vera Rubin, tying this directly to rubin-ramp and samsung-hbm. If Samsung genuinely converges HBM share toward its ~40%-class DRAM share, that is the single biggest threat to 000660's HBM price/mix premium and to the assumption that HBM stays a two-supplier oligopoly with Hynix advantaged. Confirmation would be Nvidia/AMD qualification disclosures and Samsung HBM bit-share data in 2H; refutation would be HBM4 yield or 1c-DRAM stumbles keeping Samsung's allocation concentrated in AMD rather than Nvidia.
  Sources: [en.sedaily.com](https://en.sedaily.com/finance/2026/07/30/samsung-hbm4-to-exceed-60-percent-of-hbm-sales-in-second)
- `7` **Why a Chinese Chip Stock You've Never Heard of Is Shaking Wall Street - Odaily**
  CXMT listed on the STAR Market July 27, 2026 at 8.66 yuan and closed at 49 (+466%), raising 57.92bn yuan (~$8.6bn) — Asia's largest 2026 IPO — for a ~3.3tn yuan (~$488bn) cap, with record 141bn yuan first-day turnover; the read-through knocked SanDisk -12%, WDC -7%, Micron -5%, SK Hynix ADRs -6%. The fundamentals are genuinely inflectionary but node-limited: global DRAM share 7.67% in 2025 rising to 8-9% in Q1 26, Q1 26 revenue 50.8bn yuan (+7x YoY) swinging from a 2.83bn yuan operating loss to 35.43bn yuan profit at >70% gross margin — margins earned by filling the DDR5/LPDDR5 hole the Big Three vacated for HBM. Critically for coverage: CXMT cannot mass-produce HBM, and cost/bit runs ~30% above leaders with EUV access blocked by export controls, so the direct threat to 000660's HBM4 franchise and to SNDK/285A NAND is second-order — the real risk is a state-funded, $8.6bn-war-chest price war in commodity DRAM, which sources explicitly flag as the bear case while Morgan Stanley calls the sell-off excessive. Watch CXMT's stated capex/capacity plan and 3Q26 DDR5 contract prices: a flat-to-up contract print refutes the price-war read; a first sequential decline confirms it.
  Sources: [odaily.news](https://odaily.news/en/post/5212201)
- `7` **Azure Tops $100B, Copilot Paid Seats Jump to 30M in Microsoft Blowout Quarter**
  Microsoft FQ4-26: $90B revenue, Azure crossed $100B for the fiscal year, and Microsoft 365 Copilot paid seats jumped from 20M in April to over 30M — ~10M adds in one quarter, the fastest in product history, with Nadella citing hundreds of enterprises buying millions of seats of the high-end E7 bundle. That directly contradicts the July 3 leaked memo showing <4.5% of 450M commercial M365 seats paid for Copilot and only 20–30% weekly usage; GitHub Copilot hit 50M users and appears in one of three pull requests, and Purview audited 15B+ Copilot interactions, +~360% y/y. The caveat the piece flags is decision-relevant: seats are contracted licenses, often heavily discounted in displacement deals, with no disclosed ARPU or weekly-active rate — so this is a top-of-funnel datapoint, not proven revenue. Read-through for coverage: agentic-seat monetization at this pace is a demand signal supporting Azure inference capacity (NVDA, AVGO custom silicon, VRT power/cooling) and a competitive overhang for application-layer names in the priorities list (INTU bear-watch, NOW, CRWD) — confirm with any ARPU/weekly-usage disclosure or renewal-rate commentary next quarter.
  Sources: [techtimes.com](https://techtimes.com/articles/322143/20260729/azure-tops-100b-copilot-paid-seats-jump-30m-microsoft-blowout-quarter.htm)
- `6` **AMD devient actionnaire potentiel d’Anthropic en marge d’un accord de calcul et de développement logiciel - IT SOCIAL**
  AMD–Anthropic: up to 2GW of Instinct MI450/MI455X in Helios racks with the first 1GW entering production in H1 2027, paired with up to $5B of AMD equity investment into Anthropic and a multi-year deal to use Claude to accelerate ROCm development; AMD frames the TAM at $2T for accelerators/CPUs/DC over 4–5 years including $1.4T accelerators by 2030, and the stock rose ~10% on the day. Wells Fargo kept a $615 target and said the software element raises confidence in its $40.6B 2027 AMD datacenter GPU estimate — i.e. the bull case now rests on ROCm closing the CUDA gap, with the anecdote of one Anthropic engineer wiring Claude to an MI355X rack and producing a working performance graph over a weekend. For amd-parity this is the second anchor customer after OpenAI's ~10% warrant deal and it converts Helios from roadmap to committed 2027 capacity, with pull-through for HBM4 (000660, 285A), CoWoS (TSM), and rack power/cooling (VRT); note Anthropic is deliberately multi-sourcing across Trainium and TPUs (mrvl-trainium read: no incremental Trainium news here, but Anthropic's diversification caps any single vendor's share). Caveats stated in the text: no firm timeline for gigawatts 2+ and no committed investment amount, and the structure is again vendor-financed circular demand — confirm with AMD's next DC GPU guide and MI450 tape-out/MLPerf data; refute if H1 2027 production slips or ROCm parity claims fail to show up in benchmarks.
  Sources: [itsocial.fr](https://itsocial.fr/cloud-infrastructure-it/cloud-infrastructure-it-actualites/amd-devient-actionnaire-potentiel-danthropic-en-marge-dun-accord-de-calcul-et-de-developpement-logiciel)
- `6` **Earnings call transcript: Meta misses EPS in Q2 2026 as stock sinks after hours By Investing.com**
  From the transcript: Meta raised the low end of FY26 total expense guidance to $165–169B specifically to absorb a $2.4B legal charge booked in Q2, while still committing to 2026 operating income above 2025 — so the EPS miss is partly a one-off legal item rather than pure AI-opex escalation. That distinction matters for meta-capex/META: if the market is discounting the stock on the headline profit decline (net income -14%) when $2.4B is non-recurring and the company is still guiding to operating income growth, the compute-spend trajectory is unaffected and downstream orders (NVDA, AVGO/MTIA, VRT, memory) are unchanged. The text is truncated before the capex figure, so pair it with the $130–145B guide from the other reporting. - `3` **Marvell To Invest $250M In Bengaluru, Hyderabad R&D** → [[Theses/MRVL - Marvell Technology]]
  Marvell will invest $250M in India over three years (~$83M/yr) and double its India headcount, expanding Bengaluru and Hyderabad on its 20th anniversary there; India is already its second-largest R&D organization, working on 2nm-and-beyond nodes, high-speed analog IP, subsystem design and end-to-end silicon engineering for hyperscaler infrastructure. This is an opex/engineering-capacity story, not a design-win datapoint: it says nothing directly about the mrvl-trainium or mrvl-fabric questions, but a doubling of low-cost 2nm-capable design capacity is the input required to run multiple concurrent custom-XPU and fabric programs, which is the bottleneck bears cite when arguing MRVL cedes sockets to Broadcom/Alchip. Inference: engineering headcount growth of this magnitude signals a booked pipeline management expects to staff, since headcount lands ahead of revenue by 12–24 months. Watch MRVL's opex guide and any custom-silicon revenue commitment on the next call to confirm; if opex steps up without new named programs, it reads as margin risk instead.
  Sources: [indiawest.com](https://indiawest.com/us-chipmaker-marvell-to-invest-250-million-in-bengaluru-hyderabad/)
- `1` **Gary Black Pegs TSLA's Fair Value at $312 - Tesla (NASDAQ:TSLA) - Benzinga** → [[Theses/UBER - Uber]]
  No new operating data: Gary Black models TSLA 2030 EPS of $7.75 x ~70x discounted at a 14.8% cost of equity for $312 fair value and a $250 buy price, and in the same thread said Uber 'continues to outperform' Tesla as it expands autonomous partner initiatives. For uber-av this is sentiment, not substance — it does say the 'Uber imminent demise' narrative is losing sell-side/buy-side adherents, which is a mild positive for UBER multiple support. Nothing here changes robotaxi mileage or city-expansion facts; watch Waymo/Tesla city counts and Uber's AV partner announcements for the real signal.
  Sources: [benzinga.com](https://benzinga.com/markets/tech/26/07/60787487/gary-black-pegs-tsla-fair-value-at-312)

## value

- `1` **How This Uber Options Trade Creates a Cheaper Entry Price**
  An options-income podcast argues UBER is the cheapest it has been on price-to-free-cash-flow "in quite some time" with rising margins, and that pre-earnings implied vol is elevated — they sold the Aug-21 $62 put for $0.75 against a ~$73 spot (~15% margin of safety, 14.9% annualized). The only decision-useful content is the second-order market signal: elevated UBER option premium into the print is consistent with the market pricing binary headline risk, which for this book is the Waymo/robotaxi expansion question (uber-av) rather than the rideshare P&L. Treat the valuation claim as unverified third-party commentary — no revenue, EBITDA, or bookings figures are given. Confirm or refute at the earnings date with actual FCF and any commentary on AV partnership economics and city-level Waymo overlap.
  Sources: [Johnny Hopkins](https://acquirersmultiple.com/2026/07/how-this-uber-options-trade-creates-a-cheaper-entry-price/)
- `0` **Investing in Momentum**
  Ben Carlson defends a rules-based momentum factor strategy on behavioral grounds (investors sell winners early, hold losers, then herd), quoting Fama that momentum is "the premier market anomaly." No ticker- or supply-chain-specific content; no action for coverage.
  Sources: [Ben Carlson](https://awealthofcommonsense.com/2026/07/investing-in-momentum/)

Off-coverage name with no read-through to the AI supply chain or agentic-commerce theses; no action.
